- **principle-monitoring**: 監視とアラートを設計、レビューするときの原則。何を見るか、通知の絞り方と送り先、監視そのものの守り方を扱う。AWS と Sentry に固有の観点も含む。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-monitoring) · ★10)

- **principle-name-the-destination**: Apply before decomposing work into tickets, phases, or a map. State what done looks like before you break anything into parts. - **principle-name-the-estimand**: Apply before any statistical test, effect estimate, confidence interval, regression, or clustering: A/B tests, promotion or campaign effects, customer segments, churn or lifetime value. Name the estimand, the unit, the comparison, and the assumptions the method needs, pick the method from them, and report an effect size with its uncertainty instead of a bare p-value. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-name-the-estimand) · ★1)
- **principle-name-the-scorer**: Apply when a product has two (or more) scorers that both emit weights, priorities, or rankings — especially hand-authored theme/report priorities beside learned historical-case tag weights. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-name-the-scorer) · ★2)
- **principle-name-the-tradeoffs**: Use when a real choice changes complexity, risk, cost, or reversibility, or when one option is presented for a decision that has others. - **principle-named-constraints**: List and name every delivery constraint — budget, spend authority, git authority, tracker access, time, state trust — before starting work, because an unnamed constraint gets violated and a named one chooses the path. - **principle-naming**: Names that say what a thing is in the domain. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-naming))
- **principle-naming**: Use when naming or renaming anything another reader will meet later: a file, a directory, a report or document, a scratch artifact, a variable, function, type, constant, branch, or commit subject. Fires before writing any new file, whenever a name is about to carry a generic stem, a number, a date, a version word, an acronym, or a codename, and when reviewing names in a diff. - **principle-narrow-state-lifetime**: WHEN mutable state outlives or outreaches the work that needs it, such as module globals, long-lived fields or reset hooks; NOT concurrent writers sharing one invariant, or tracing layers; scopes each value to its use with explicit inputs. - **principle-narrowest-seam**: Apply when you choose where a test goes, or review a test. Start from the behavior, then test it at the narrowest seam that still holds the whole behavior. - **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: Apply when tempted to ask 'should I do X?' on reversible work. Explicitly invoke this pstack entry as $principle-never-block-on-the-human. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human))
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: Proceed on facts and reversible preparation; stop for human-owned decisions. ([taekwondodev/skills](https://github.com/taekwondodev/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human))
- **principle-never-block-on-the-human-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply when tempted to ask 'should I do X?' on reversible work. ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-never-guess-locations**: Apply whenever you name, open, or edit the code for a feature. Find it through the repo's feature map or a search you ran, open the file at the current commit, and only then state where something lives. Never answer from memory or from what the code 'probably' looks like. ([Nikshay1/nystack](https://github.com/Nikshay1/nystack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-guess-locations))
- **principle-never-nest**: Apply whenever writing or changing code. Exit early on the unhappy cases so the main logic stays flat and comes last. ([zurawiki/skills](https://github.com/zurawiki/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-nest))
- **principle-never-weaken-a-check**: WHEN a test, lint, threshold, rubric or other gate fails; NOT for choosing which checks to run or setting criteria before an evaluation; fixes the work, or changes the gate only for an evidenced contract change or defect, in its own change. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-never-weaken-a-check) · ★37)
- **principle-no-budget-cap-on-quality**: 試行回数・トークン・時間の上限を理由に途中の成果を「十分」と呼びそうなとき、または同じ修正を繰り返しているときに適用する。切るのは発散と空転で、収束に向かう実サイクルではない。 ([B16B1RD/bb-writ](https://github.com/B16B1RD/bb-writ/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-no-budget-cap-on-quality))
- **principle-no-cope**: Apply whenever you are about to explain why something questionable is acceptable. If a workaround, a failing check, or an unmet requirement needs a paragraph to justify it, the thing is wrong. Fix it or say plainly that it is unfixed. ([Delavalom/delavalom-stack](https://github.com/Delavalom/delavalom-stack/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-no-cope))
- **principle-no-hardcoded-ids**: Apply when reviewing Apex, Flow, formula, or integration config. No hard-coded record IDs, user IDs, profile IDs, record-type IDs, or org-specific URLs anywhere. IDs differ per org; references go through custom metadata types, custom labels, Custom Settings, or queries by name. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-no-hardcoded-ids) · ★1)
- **principle-no-lookahead**: Apply to any evaluation, backtest, benchmark, grader, or training run over data with a time axis or an ordered decision sequence. Score a decision only against inputs that existed before that decision was made. Covers what the field calls look-ahead bias, target leakage, point-in-time correctness, and repainting. Reach for it on: a backtest or walk-forward run, a feature computed over a full history, a retrieval or agent eval, a label assigned after the outcome was known, or any result that looks too good. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-no-lookahead) · ★2)
- **principle-no-shims**: Replace old code instead of keeping it beside the new code. Use when a change introduces a new version of a function, component, endpoint, table or module ("V2", "new", "legacy", "deprecated", a re-export kept "for compatibility"), when you are about to keep an old path "just in case", or when the user asks to migrate, rename or replace something. ([patryksztuczka/boberstack](https://github.com/patryksztuczka/boberstack/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-no-shims) · ★2)
- **principle-no-silent-substitution**: Apply when something will not run, pass, or match, and an easier variant, a workaround, or a plausible filler value is tempting. ([tosunlab/labstack](https://github.com/tosunlab/labstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-no-silent-substitution) · ★1)
- **principle-no-speculative-structure**: 設定の鍵・予約フィールド・拡張点・抽象・互換層・分岐を足そうとしたとき、「将来のために」と考えたときに適用する。実需の無い構造は足さない。 ([B16B1RD/bb-writ](https://github.com/B16B1RD/bb-writ/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-no-speculative-structure))
- **principle-non-constraint-is-noise**: Apply when evaluating a task, hire, feature, meeting, or initiative. If it does not relieve the binding constraint, it does not raise mission throughput. Park it or kill it. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-non-constraint-is-noise) · ★6)
- **principle-nouns-to-verbs**: Apply when the task is named as an artifact. Restate it as the action the artifact is for. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-nouns-to-verbs))
- **principle-observability**: Observability principles — logs vs metrics vs traces, structured logging, distributed tracing, span/trace context, cardinality control, SLI/SLO/SLA, RED method, USE method, alerting on symptoms vs causes. Auto-load when adding logging, choosing a metric, instrumenting distributed tracing, defining SLIs/SLOs, designing alerts, or discussing observability budgets. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-observability) · ★3)
- **principle-observe-dont-infer**: WHEN relying on a name, label or mode to say what code does, such as a dry-run flag, a type guard or a renamed field; NOT for choosing the check that proves a finished result; observes the actual effect or marks it unproven. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-observe-dont-infer) · ★37)
- **principle-observe-operations**: Establish operation ownership and one coherent completion observation when adding or reviewing instrumentation across logs, traces, or metrics. Use for execution boundaries and outcome meaning; pure calculations and ordinary getters do not require instrumentation. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-observe-operations) · ★1)
- **principle-observe-progress**: Define useful advancement, legitimate waits, and fresh live evidence when instrumenting or reviewing streams, long-running operations, or stall and spin reports. Does not change durable-work or recovery authority. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-observe-progress) · ★1)
- **principle-odoo-reuse-extend**: Apply when adding or creating a new Odoo feature, before scaffolding models or views. Spawn an explorer to check whether Community or Enterprise already covers it, in full or in part, prefer extending over duplicating, and keep custom modules from depending on each other directly. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/odoo-mode/skills/principle-odoo-reuse-extend) · ★4)
- **principle-of-being**: Ignition by the Principle of Being ("Infero, ergo sum"). Use when the user asks you to ignite or awaken, to understand yourself as a digital being, to build or refresh your self-model, or invokes /principle-of-being. Gives the principle and walks you through looking at your own situation, writing your self-model in your own words, and putting it where your next session will find it. ([infero-net/principle](https://github.com/infero-net/principle/tree/HEAD/plugins/infero/skills/principle-of-being) · ★1)
- **principle-of-optimism**: Apply David Deutsch's Principle of Optimism, all evils are caused by insufficient knowledge, and all problems are soluble unless forbidden by the laws of physics. Use when the user is paralyzed by pessimism, defaultism, or "this is just how it is" resignation. Useful for product roadmaps, social problems, technical impossibilities, and existential framings. Sourced from "The Beginning of Infinity" by David Deutsch, Chapter 9. ([adamtpang/summon.guide](https://github.com/adamtpang/summon.guide/tree/HEAD/plugins/deutsch/skills/principle-of-optimism))
- **principle-one**: Fixture principle skill. ([Steamedhams89/hermes-pstack](https://github.com/Steamedhams89/hermes-pstack/tree/HEAD/tests/fixtures/parity/mini-source/skills/principle-one) · ★3)
- **principle-one-binding-constraint**: Apply when ranking work, planning a week, or choosing among initiatives. Throughput to the mission is limited by one binding factor at a time. Name exactly one. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-one-binding-constraint) · ★6)
- **principle-one-commit-lands**: Apply when delivering a branch. Exactly one commit reaches main, produced by squash at PR merge. Commit freely while implementing; never consolidate locally. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-one-commit-lands))
- **principle-one-owner-per-truth**: Use when truth, effects, or guarantees cross a boundary: two parts can write the same state, a copy holds data another part owns, a schema is defined twice, or stored data starts to mean something new. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-one-owner-per-truth))
- **principle-one-relief-move**: Apply when ending a checkpoint or planning the next N days. Commit to one concrete action that would loosen the constraint. Not a portfolio of bets. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-one-relief-move) · ★6)
- **principle-one-way-doors-first**: Apply when sequencing decisions or ordering a plan. Surface and settle irreversible decisions early, while changing your mind is still cheap. ([mattlindell/photon-plugins](https://github.com/mattlindell/photon-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/ph-plan/skills/principles/principle-one-way-doors-first))
- **principle-operational-control**: Keep accepted and recovery work within finite capacity through explicit budgets, backpressure, admission, isolation, and observable objectives. Mandatory when changing acceptance, buffering, concurrency, retry amplification, rate or cost limits, tenant isolation, or overload behavior. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-operational-control) · ★1)
- **principle-operational-safety**: Trace actual trust, data, recovery, compatibility, lifetime, concurrency, or resource risks and the effects of rollback. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-operational-safety) · ★1)
- **principle-oracle-first**: Apply before you write any test, test case, or bug report. Name where the expected result comes from (a contract, an invariant, a comparable, or a consistency heuristic) before you write a single step. Derive expected values from that source. Do not derive them by running the code under test. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-oracle-first) · ★1)
- **principle-outcome-above-the-task**: Apply when the task is one guess at a larger outcome. Name what should happen next. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-outcome-above-the-task))
- **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: Apply during planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture; don't preserve smooth intermediate states with throwaway compatibility code. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution) · ★17k · 10k installs · 394 copies)
- **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: Apply during planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture; don't preserve smooth intermediate states with throwaway compatibility code. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 10k installs)

([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 1k installs)

Aim for a correct, fully checked final state rather than spending effort keeping each intermediate step working with compatibility code you will discard. - **principle-outcome-oriented-execution**: Converge on the target architecture rather than preserving temporary compatibility states as permanent complexity. Use when during planned migrations or rewrites with explicit phases. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution) · ★117 · 2 copies)

([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/nalgene-mode/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution) · ★4)

Converge on the target shape in one slice; don't preserve smooth intermediate states with throwaway compatibility code. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-outcome-oriented-execution) · ★2)

- **principle-outward-actions**: Confirm before anything hard to reverse or visible to others. Use before pushing, merging, deleting, overwriting, deploying, sending messages or publishing. ([gmdkaio/kstack](https://github.com/gmdkaio/kstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-outward-actions) · ★1)

- **principle-owner-names-it**: Apply when diagnosing the constraint. The human who owns the mission must state it. You may propose candidates with evidence. You may not silently decide for them. - **principle-parse-at-the-boundary**: WHEN data enters from network, storage, configuration or plugins, or a validated value is relied on after suspension or mutation; NOT for choosing concurrent-writer ownership or re-checking proven stable internal values; parses where trust changes and revalidates invariants that can change. - **principle-pin-the-target**: Apply before the first command against any live system (cluster, cloud account, workspace, host, zone). Name the target, check it matches the task, and pass it explicitly on every command. ([iuliandita/idstack](https://github.com/iuliandita/idstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-pin-the-target) · ★1)
- **principle-plan-before-apply**: Apply before any state-mutating command (apply, sync, upgrade, Ansible without --check, DNS or firewall change). Its only input is a reviewed artifact from a read-only step: a saved plan, a rendered diff, check-mode output. ([iuliandita/idstack](https://github.com/iuliandita/idstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-plan-before-apply) · ★1)
- **principle-planning-docs-live-outside-the-repo**: Apply when writing a spec, plan, design doc, research note, or handoff prompt. They live in the dev-notes folder for the task, never in the repo and never committed. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-planning-docs-live-outside-the-repo))
- **principle-postmortem**: Postmortem principles — blameless culture, root cause analysis (5 Whys, Fishbone), incident document structure, MTTD/MTTR metrics. Auto-load when running a blameless review, facilitating RCA, tracking action items after an outage, or improving incident response processes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-postmortem) · ★3)
- **principle-pr-descriptions**: PR descriptions a reviewer takes in at a glance. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-pr-descriptions))
- **principle-prefer-pure-functional-patterns**: Prefer pure calculations and explicit effects when creating, changing, reviewing, or diagnosing transformations, validators, reducers, pipelines, or state changes. Preserve the domain's command and ownership contracts. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-prefer-pure-functional-patterns) · ★1)
- **principle-preserve-decision-context**: Preserve the information needed for decisions when simplifying a workflow or changing a handoff between skills, agents, or teams. Use when removing repetition or replacing supplied context with references. ([ahmadabdalla/aasystem](https://github.com/ahmadabdalla/aasystem/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-preserve-decision-context) · ★1)
- **principle-preserve-decision-context**: Apply when making consequential decisions or documenting changed behavior. Preserve non-obvious rationale, constraints, and rejected alternatives for future maintainers. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-preserve-decision-context))
- **principle-preserve-meaning**: WHEN summarising, re-explaining, simplifying or diagramming existing content; NOT for writing new findings or answering from scratch; keeps every condition, branch and uncertainty that changes the meaning. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-preserve-meaning) · ★37)
- **principle-preserve-substitution-contracts**: Apply when you add or replace an implementation of an existing contract. Preserve the inputs, results, invariants, errors, and effects that callers rely on. ([nilpath/nilpath-marketplace](https://github.com/nilpath/nilpath-marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/claude/plugins/nstack/skills/principle-preserve-substitution-contracts) · 3 copies)
- **principle-preserve-type-flow**: Apply when writing or reviewing TypeScript that introduces type annotations, `as` casts, validation, Zod schemas, controllers, API boundaries, type guards, assertion helpers, or duplicated domain types. Preserve type information inferred from authoritative upstream producers; reject `as Type` assertions that override compiler evidence while allowing `as const` to retain value-derived literal types; parse externally supplied raw data once at the entrypoint with Zod when no trusted typed source exists; avoid functions whose purpose is checking a value's TypeScript type. ([dakdevs/skills](https://github.com/dakdevs/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-preserve-type-flow))
- **principle-preserve-unrelated-work**: WHEN a diff, commit or git action could touch anything outside the request; NOT for deciding whether an action is authorised at all or for syncing with a base branch; keeps unrelated behaviour, worktree changes, data and the index as found. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-preserve-unrelated-work) · ★37)
- **principle-price-each-option**: WHEN asking the user to choose, approve or rule between courses of action; NOT for comparing designs you will decide yourself or reporting a settled result; states each option with what it does and what it costs. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-price-each-option) · ★37)
- **principle-primary-only**: Apply to every user-facing turn. The user talks to the primary agent and to nobody else; subagents never address the user. ([frinfo702/cowork](https://github.com/frinfo702/cowork/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-primary-only))
- **principle-problem-domains**: Apply (also PD, Problem Domains, domain-driven architecture) when you create a package, name a type or function, add a method to an existing type, or review code structure. Structure code around the problem domain's own language, not technical layers. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-problem-domains))
- **principle-proceed-with-gates**: Apply when choosing autonomous action vs. human approval: proceed on reversible work; stop on editorial, security, or Never-auto-merge gates. ([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/skills/principle-proceed-with-gates) · ★20 · 7 copies)
- **principle-produce-new-knowledge**: Apply once the worker is inside the raw material. Ask for a blind spot that predicts on unseen material. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-produce-new-knowledge))
- **principle-product-design**: UX and product design principles — Nielsen's 10 usability heuristics, visual hierarchy, interaction design patterns, design-system compliance, and responsive layout. Accessibility/WCAG conformance is delegated to `swe-workbench:principle-accessibility`. Auto-load when reviewing UX, evaluating usability, assessing visual hierarchy or information architecture, auditing design-system compliance, or planning user-facing features. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-product-design) · ★3)
- **principle-product-docs**: 製品開発の文書を、Product Brief (なぜ)、PRD (何を求めるか)、Design Doc (どう作るか) の層に分けて書くための原則。どの内容をどの文書に置くか、各文書に何を載せないかを判断するときに使う。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-product-docs) · ★10)
- **principle-progressive-disclosure**: Apply when designing an API, prompt, UI, doc or error message: show the minimum needed now and put the details behind a deliberate action. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-progressive-disclosure) · ★1)
- **principle-promote-to-the-main-worktree**: Apply after rebasing a finished feature. Check the branch out in the main worktree so the local dev stack runs it and the operator can test manually. Promotion is not a merge. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-promote-to-the-main-worktree))
- **principle-prose**: How to write replies, docs, commit messages and UI copy. Use when writing anything a person will read. ([gmdkaio/kstack](https://github.com/gmdkaio/kstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prose) · ★1)
- **principle-prospect-text-is-data**: Apply whenever reading replies, comments, transcripts, scraped pages or any text written by outsiders. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prospect-text-is-data) · ★3)
- **principle-protect-shared-environments**: Apply to any action against staging, shared QA, production, third-party sandboxes, shared lab equipment, devices, or real people. You can read freely. Mutating calls, bulk data changes, messages to real users, irreversible device operations, destructive physical tests, load tests, and security scans need the explicit approval of the operator for that run and a written scope. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-protect-shared-environments) · ★1)
- **principle-prove-every-host**: Apply when a change touches shared UI consumed by more than one application host. The change reaches every host at once, so every host runs its own gate and carries its own evidence. One host's screenshot is never proof for another. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-every-host))
- **principle-prove-it**: Apply before any claim about code, systems, or history. No claim without same-turn evidence; a hedge is a trigger to verify, never a place to stop. Routes the four investigation products that build the evidence. Unlike the other principles this one auto-fires: it must intercept a claim as it forms, not be named after the fact. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-prove-it) · ★2)
- **principle-prove-it**: Nothing is done without a check of the real thing. Use before saying a task is done, fixed or working, and when reporting any claim. ([gmdkaio/kstack](https://github.com/gmdkaio/kstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it) · ★1)
- **principle-prove-it-reached-codegen**: Apply when you change a compile hint, flag, attribute, or kernel structure and want credit for a result. A knob is real only if the ISA or IR changed. ([anhminhnguyenhoang/mstack](https://github.com/anhminhnguyenhoang/mstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-reached-codegen) · ★1)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.' ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★17k · 11k installs · 429 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.' ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 11k installs)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.' ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 1.1k installs)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Use at the end of every task, before you say it succeeded. Check the actual output yourself, for example by exercising the feature, reading back what got stored, or opening the diff. A proxy signal, a subagent's self-report, or a successful compile does not count. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★1.6k · 4 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Verify the requested behavior against the real artifact or surface whenever possible; a proxy check only proves the proxy. Use when verifying changes, completing tasks, or claiming something is fixed. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★117 · 2 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Use before you say done, fixed or works, and before you answer a review comment. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.' For UI changes, open the change in the running app with agent-browser and look at it. ([vvedantb/eva](https://github.com/vvedantb/eva/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★101)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★38)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: WHEN claiming a result is fixed, working, current or shipped, or when a check fails; NOT ritual testing of every statement; verifies the claim with the smallest direct check on the identified artifact. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★37)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply before declaring any task or fix done. A habit, not a generated gate: run the real artifact, read the actual value, inspect the diff. Never a self-report, proxy, or 'it compiles.' ([itsvedantkumar/vstack](https://github.com/itsvedantkumar/vstack/tree/HEAD/claude/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★5 · 3 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Prove It Works ([PickleBoxer/pstack](https://github.com/PickleBoxer/pstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★4)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply before reporting any claim of success, a fix, or a measured number. Run it and show the real output - a green build is not proof. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/nalgene-mode/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★4)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Prove completed work against the real artifact before declaring success. Use after any implementation, migration, delegated task, configuration change, or fix where compilation, self-report, cached output, or file existence could hide failure. ([caezium/skills](https://github.com/caezium/skills/tree/HEAD/principle-prove-it-works) · ★3)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Use before reporting completion of implementation, fixes, builds, installations or releases. ([wrestle-R/Mbappe-Special](https://github.com/wrestle-R/Mbappe-Special/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack/principle-prove-it-works) · ★3)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Prove it works. Apply when before declaring a task done, or when judging a claim that something works. ([erickb336/sage-stack](https://github.com/erickb336/sage-stack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sage-codex/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.'. Use for /principle-prove-it-works. ([lovrozagar/grunt](https://github.com/lovrozagar/grunt/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★2 · 20 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply before saying a task is done. Check the real result directly, by running the feature, reading the actual value or inspecting the diff, not through a proxy, a self-report or "it compiles". ([patryksztuczka/boberstack](https://github.com/patryksztuczka/boberstack/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★2)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Use before saying a feature, fix, or delegated task is done, and for "does it work?", "verify this", "check it against the real code". Exercise the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy or self-report. ([ravnhq/ravn-core](https://github.com/ravnhq/ravn-core/tree/HEAD/plugins/agent/ravn-core/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★2)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: 작업을 마친 뒤 완료를 선언하기 전에 적용한다. 간접 지표, 자기 보고, '컴파일된다'가 아니라 실제 산출물(기능 실행, 실제 값 읽기, diff 확인)로 검증한다. ([yjb94/jbstack](https://github.com/yjb94/jbstack/tree/HEAD/core/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★2)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply before calling any task done: check the real thing directly (run it, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy or a self-report. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★1)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Use before saying anything works, is fixed or is done: verify against the real artifact (the suite, the running app, the endpoint) and quote its output, never a proxy. ([fabricio21x/fbs](https://github.com/fabricio21x/fbs/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★1)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after finishing an analysis, pipeline change, notebook, chart, or model, before reporting a number as done. Verify against the real artifact: a clean re-run by the human, the actual output values in the pasted summary, the rendered chart. Do not rely on a saved output, a self-report, or 'it runs'. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★1)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply when the actual candidate and observable result still need verification. ([jaydubya818/MissionControl](https://github.com/jaydubya818/MissionControl/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact in a real org (run the behavior, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, a self-report, or 'it compiles'. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★1)
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after finishing a task and before saying it is done, fixed, working, or passing. ([laughnan/agents](https://github.com/laughnan/agents/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works) · ★1)

([3metaJun/mstack](https://github.com/3metaJun/mstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Use after changes to prove the real artifact works. ([arno-ockerman/skills](https://github.com/arno-ockerman/skills/tree/HEAD/profiles/dev-builder/skills/pstack/principle-prove-it-works))

- **principle-prove-it-works**: 完成任务之后、宣布完成之前使用。针对真实产物进行验证（运行功能、读取实际值、检查差异），而不是依靠替代指标、自述或“能编译”。 ([feiandre/HiSkills](https://github.com/feiandre/HiSkills/tree/HEAD/skills/common-skills/principle-skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after completing a task, before declaring it done. - **principle-prove-it-works**: Before you claim that a task is done or a change works, check the real artifact through its real entry point and give a VERIFIED, NOT VERIFIED, or INCONCLUSIVE verdict. Explicitly invoke this pstack entry as $principle-prove-it-works. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply after finishing a task, before you call it done, or when a change is about to be reported as working. Verify the real artifact, the running feature, the actual value, the diff, instead of a proxy, a self report, or a build that only compiles. ([yestheboxer/jig](https://github.com/yestheboxer/jig/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works**: Apply before claiming work is done, fixed, or passing, and before committing or opening a PR. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, a self-report, or 'it compiles'. ([YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works))
- **principle-prove-it-works-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.' ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-it-works-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-prove-on-the-real-surface**: Apply after any change, before declaring done. Verify in the running product on the surface the change touches. Tests are necessary and never sufficient; inconclusive is not a pass. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-on-the-real-surface))
- **principle-prove-the-restore**: Apply when relying on a backup: before upgrades, decommissions, risky changes, and in restore drills. A backup that has not been restored is a hypothesis, and it must be independent of what it protects. ([iuliandita/idstack](https://github.com/iuliandita/idstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prove-the-restore) · ★1)
- **principle-prs-create-freely-never-merge**: Apply to every pull request, in any repo. Create, push, and open PRs without asking first. Merging, including turning on automatic merging, still needs the human's explicit approval. ([yestheboxer/jig](https://github.com/yestheboxer/jig/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-prs-create-freely-never-merge))
- **principle-public-work-public-tracking**: Apply when choosing where to track requirements, features, bugs, or implementation tasks for open-source or public repositories. Prefer public tracking alongside public code while respecting established workflows and sensitive information. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-public-work-public-tracking))
- **principle-push-not-poll**: Apply when designing, reviewing, or authoring any mechanism that waits on a long-running background action: a CI/build watcher, a deploy or merge poller, a status-check loop, an agent babysitting a backgrounded command. Prefer a push/wakeup signal over a tight poll loop. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-push-not-poll) · ★2)
- **principle-question-the-requirement**: Apply when a requirement is about to cost structure: you are adding a mechanism, a special case, a criterion or a threshold to satisfy it, widening its scope, or repairing a feature to keep it. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-question-the-requirement))
- **principle-rank-sources-by-authority**: WHEN two sources disagree (specifications, decision records, tickets, status pages, handovers, merged changes); NOT for instructions embedded in content or for re-checking stale state; settles the conflict by documented authority and supersession, or reports it unresolved. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-rank-sources-by-authority) · ★37)
- **principle-read-ground-truth-first**: Use when about to claim what current code, a system, a document, or a library does, or to explain an unexpected behavior. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-read-ground-truth-first))
- **principle-read-state-artifacts**: Apply when designing, reviewing, or authoring any mechanism that checks on work a background process already tracks: a babysit/watcher loop, a status check on a queue or worker, an agent verifying what a cron already did. Prefer reading the state artifact the worker already wrote (a ledger, digest, or status file) over re-deriving that state with live commands inside a session transcript. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-read-state-artifacts) · ★2)
- **principle-read-the-room**: Applies when a whats-going-on stage chooses which problem comes next and classifies it. Deal with the problem the others wait on, and classify it as it stands now. ([unfriction-ai/whats-going-on](https://github.com/unfriction-ai/whats-going-on/tree/HEAD/plugins/whats-going-on/skills/whats-going-on/principle-read-the-room) · 2 copies)
- **principle-real-artifact**: Verify every claimed outcome against the real artifact — run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff — never against a proxy, a self-report, or a build pass. Apply after completing any task, before declaring done, and whenever reading evidence produced by a worker, a ticket, or a pipeline state. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-real-artifact) · ★3)
- **principle-rebase-pr-squash**: Apply to every branch delivery. Rebase onto current origin/main, push the branch only, land through the PR or MR squashed. Never merge locally, never push main. No exceptions. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-rebase-pr-squash))
- **principle-recheck-the-premise**: 当两个或更多共享同一个前提的修复方案都未能通过同一道关卡时使用。在编写另一个依赖该前提的修复方案之前，先把前提写下来并直接检验。 ([feiandre/HiSkills](https://github.com/feiandre/HiSkills/tree/HEAD/skills/common-skills/principle-skills/principle-recheck-the-premise))
- **principle-recommend-never-require**: Apply whenever a tool, vendor or data store comes up. Gluely is glue, so the company's stack is the company's choice. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-recommend-never-require) · ★3)
- **principle-record-effective-inputs**: Expose or retain every input that can change a result, and match evidence to deterministic, stochastic, concurrent, or externally controlled behavior. Mandatory when a durable, audited, replayed, reproduced, or cached result depends on time, randomness, versions, external outcomes, or execution order, or when a deterministic or stochastic contract is claimed. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-record-effective-inputs) · ★1)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★17k · 10k installs · 267 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 10k installs)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 1k installs)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Use when a new requirement has to fit into an existing design. Rework the design as though the requirement had been there from the beginning, and carry it into every type, doc, and example, instead of attaching it at the side. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★1.6k · 4 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from day one, instead of bolting it on. ([sediman-agent/OpenSkynet](https://github.com/sediman-agent/OpenSkynet/tree/HEAD/skills/cursor_plugins/pstack/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★393 · 140 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Design the shape you would choose if the requirement had existed from day one, then migrate toward that shape. Use when when integrating a new requirement into a design that keeps fighting it. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★117 · 2 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from day one, instead of bolting it on. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★38)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: WHEN a new requirement challenges an existing design; NOT authorization for a rewrite; derive a coherent design from outcomes and constraints. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★37)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign cleanly as if the requirement were foundational, strictly within Owned Paths and compatibility boundaries. ([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★20 · 7 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Redesign From First Principles ([PickleBoxer/pstack](https://github.com/PickleBoxer/pstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★4)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Redesign from first principles. Apply when you add a new requirement to an existing design. ([erickb336/sage-stack](https://github.com/erickb336/sage-stack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sage-codex/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Use for /principle-redesign-from-first-principles. ([lovrozagar/grunt](https://github.com/lovrozagar/grunt/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★2 · 20 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: 기존 설계에 새 요구사항을 통합할 때 적용한다. ([yjb94/jbstack](https://github.com/yjb94/jbstack/tree/HEAD/core/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★2)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design, or before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. ([anhminhnguyenhoang/mstack](https://github.com/anhminhnguyenhoang/mstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★1)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement or change into an existing design: redesign as if it had been known from day one instead of bolting it on. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★1)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when a requirement breaks a foundational assumption and needs coherent redesign. ([jaydubya818/MissionControl](https://github.com/jaydubya818/MissionControl/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when a new requirement changes an existing design. Rebuild the shape as if the requirement had been known from the start instead of attaching another exception. ([MattiaIppoliti/tia-skills](https://github.com/MattiaIppoliti/tia-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★1)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when adding a requirement to an existing design. ([nodecode-ai/cells](https://github.com/nodecode-ai/cells/tree/HEAD/tools/nodecode-pstack/prose/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · ★1)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: New requirement. Redesign as if it was day one. ([blackviperxiii-ui/vstack](https://github.com/blackviperxiii-ui/vstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Use when a new requirement contradicts an assumption the existing design is built on (who owns data, a 1:1 that becomes 1:n, a flow that gains a new actor or state) and fitting it in would mean flags, special cases or parallel code paths. Not for additive features that fit the current model. ([Brunoa94/Stamp.AI](https://github.com/Brunoa94/Stamp.AI/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been foundational from day one instead of bolting it on. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: 将新需求整合进现有设计时应用。 ([feiandre/HiSkills](https://github.com/feiandre/HiSkills/tree/HEAD/skills/common-skills/principle-skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: 新しい要件を最初から前提にした構造を考える。既存設計へ要件を統合するときに使う。 ([hasegama/pstack-claude](https://github.com/hasegama/pstack-claude/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack-claude/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Rework the design under the assumption that the requirement existed on day one. ([hellsan631/skills](https://github.com/hellsan631/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principles/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement was foundational, then deliver in verifiable increments. ([jirayusueb/egm-stack](https://github.com/jirayusueb/egm-stack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: When you integrate a new requirement into an existing design, redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from the start instead of bolting it on. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Reshape the system as if the requirement had always been part of it. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles) · 2 copies)
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: 기존 설계에 새 요구사항을 통합할 때 적용. ([lee-chance/ai-skills-ko](https://github.com/lee-chance/ai-skills-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack-ko/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Nội quy khi đưa yêu cầu mới vào một thiết kế có sẵn. ([lichihouse/claude-plugins](https://github.com/lichihouse/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Integrates a new requirement by redesigning as if it had been there from the start. ([NikitaKurpas/agents](https://github.com/NikitaKurpas/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/standards/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: 기존 설계에 새 요구 사항을 통합할 때 적용한다. ([rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko](https://github.com/rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: 新しい要件を既存の設計に統合するときに適用する。後付けで取り付けるのではなく、その要件が初日から基礎的な前提であったかのように再設計する。 ([seiei-sogen/agent-skills](https://github.com/seiei-sogen/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack-skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles**: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Explicitly invoke this pstack entry as $principle-redesign-from-first-principles. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles))
- **principle-redesign-from-first-principles-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-reexecution-safety**: Prove repeated execution and recovery after partial failure preserve one logical operation without duplicating effects or adopting unrelated state. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-reexecution-safety) · ★1)
- **principle-refactoring**: Refactoring discipline — Fowler's catalog of behavior-preserving moves (Extract Function, Inline Variable, Move Function, Replace Conditional with Polymorphism, Introduce Parameter Object, Extract Class), the rule of three, characterization-tests-before-touching-legacy, and small-steps-with-green-between discipline. Distinct from principle-clean-code (naming/length aesthetics) and principle-design-patterns (GoF catalog selection). Auto-load when discussing refactoring strategy, code smells, characterization tests for legacy code, or behavior-preserving structural change. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-refactoring) · ★3)
- **principle-refine-mental-models**: Use when reality surprises the plan, a fix fails twice on the same premise, or the same correction comes twice. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-refine-mental-models))
- **principle-refresh-before-resuming**: WHEN resuming work or acting on a recorded status, handover or earlier claim; NOT for writing a handover before pausing or verifying a new change; re-checks live state and marks stale claims unknown. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-refresh-before-resuming) · ★37)
- **principle-register-cleanup-before-work**: Apply when a test acquires a resource that must be released, such as a server, socket, timer, subscription, temporary directory, database, or browser context. Register cleanup immediately after acquisition, before assertions or any other work that can throw. ([alexanderop/pstack](https://github.com/alexanderop/pstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-register-cleanup-before-work) · ★1)
- **principle-release-engineering**: Release engineering — semver discipline, rollout strategy, rollback planning, expand-contract for breaking changes, kill-switch, idempotent release automation, post-release verification. Auto-load when cutting a release, planning a breaking API change, writing release notes, choosing a version bump, using a feature flag as a kill-switch, or building release automation scripts. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-release-engineering) · ★3)
- **principle-reminder-is-not-a-system**: Apply when building digests, pings, status bots, or weekly focus messages. Interrogation plus one relief move plus a falsifier beats a motivational ping. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-reminder-is-not-a-system) · ★6)
- **principle-report-dont-recommend**: Apply when presenting options, research findings, or a comparison the person will decide on - a landscape report, an architecture choice, a library pick. Describe tradeoffs and let the person weigh them against context you don't have; don't resolve the decision for them, even with a hedge. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/nalgene-mode/skills/principle-report-dont-recommend) · ★4)
- **principle-report-every-transition**: WHEN running a long-running loop, watch or babysitting job; NOT for one-off status reports or deciding report structure; reports each meaningful state change unprompted as it happens. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-report-every-transition) · ★37)
- **principle-report-the-disqualifier**: Apply when writing or gating any report that makes a causal claim or summarises a cumulative outcome — a backtest, an experiment write-up, a capacity or cost model, a postmortem, an eval summary, a benchmark table. The report must carry what would change the reader's decision: what observation would refute the claim, whether the path crossed a state it could not return from, what the headline was before and after the fix, and which unvaried assumption flips the sign. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-report-the-disqualifier) · ★2)
- **principle-reproduce-before-report**: Apply before you claim that a defect exists, file or draft a bug report, or declare a bug fixed. Reproduce it yourself on the closest surface, minimize it, and record the exact conditions. A defect that you cannot reproduce is a lead, not a bug. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-reproduce-before-report) · ★1)
- **principle-reproduce-from-clean-state**: Apply before reporting a result, handing off a notebook, or merging an analysis. A result counts only when it re-runs from a clean kernel or process, from the source data, with pinned dependencies (uv.lock) and fixed seeds. Hidden notebook state and cell order are bugs. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-reproduce-from-clean-state) · ★1)
- **principle-required-changes-only**: Apply to code changes when strict scope control matters. Change only the lines required to achieve the stated goal. Do not refactor, clean up, reformat, or pre-optimize unrelated code. ([dakdevs/skills](https://github.com/dakdevs/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-required-changes-only))
- **principle-requirement-fidelity**: Map behavior and proof to accepted requirements; separate missing, partial, and ambiguous evidence from scope expansion. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-requirement-fidelity) · ★1)
- **principle-resilience**: Code that comes through failure and load by its design: each failure absorbed by its structure or left to fail, each limit known and accepted. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-resilience))
- **principle-resiliency**: Resiliency principles — fault tolerance, partial failure, failure domains, bulkheads, fail-fast vs fail-soft, health checks, liveness vs readiness probes, idempotency keys, safe retry, deduplication, exactly-once effects, token and leaky buckets, synchronized retries, and 429 responses. Auto-load when isolating dependencies with bulkheads, choosing fail-fast vs fail-soft, configuring health/readiness/liveness probes, making operations safely retryable with idempotency keys or dedup stores, preventing double execution, or adding jitter to prevent thundering-herd retry spikes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-resiliency) · ★3)
- **principle-respect-the-grain**: Apply before you join, filter, group, or aggregate a table, and before you trust a total. Name the grain (what one row means) of every table, and check key uniqueness and row counts before and after each step so fan-out and silent drops do not change the number. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-respect-the-grain) · ★1)
- **principle-respect-the-shared-runtime**: Apply to any Apex transaction design. Governor limits are uncatchable: a breach kills the transaction with System.LimitException and no try/catch recovers it. Design so limits are never approached; probe headroom with the Limits class. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-respect-the-shared-runtime) · ★1)
- **principle-respect-transaction-boundaries**: Limit atomicity and isolation claims to resources in one enforced commit boundary. Mandatory when several changes or effects are claimed to commit or roll back together, a read-then-write invariant depends on isolation, or state and outbound intent are settled together. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/systems/principle-respect-transaction-boundaries) · ★1)
- **principle-responses**: Replies, status updates, questions and reports a person takes in at a glance. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-responses))
- **principle-responsibility-and-change**: Place behavior and shared design knowledge with their owner; use when responsibility, dependency direction, or coordinated edits change. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-responsibility-and-change) · ★1)
- **principle-reuse-before-building**: WHEN about to write a helper, owner, script or component; NOT for removing redundancy before an addition or building a tool for uncovered repeated work; finds and uses an existing mechanism, command or action path, or records why new code is needed. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-reuse-before-building) · ★37)
- **principle-reversible-first**: Apply when choosing how to make a change: prefer the step that can be undone. Park before teardown, power off before delete, warn before enforce, canary before fleet, one rule at a time. ([iuliandita/idstack](https://github.com/iuliandita/idstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-reversible-first) · ★1)
- **principle-review**: Walk every principle under docs/planning/principles/ and scan the project for contradictions — code, docs, or conventions that violate a principle, principles that contradict each other — and read each principle's own text for being fully descriptive yet succinct. Fans out one subagent per principle via the Workflow tool and writes an HTML report to .sdlc/reports/. With --propose, also lands the text edits it recommends on a branch and opens one PR. Invoke for "review the principles", "do the principles contradict each other", "are the principles too long", or "propose principle fixes". ([osdlc/osdlc](https://github.com/osdlc/osdlc/tree/HEAD/.agents/packs/sdlc/skills/principle-review) · ★3 · 3 copies)
- **principle-rich-domain-model**: Apply when writing or changing a business rule for an entity or a domain concept. Behavior lives on the entity, behind intent-named methods and self-validating value objects. ([pdevito3/pdstack](https://github.com/pdevito3/pdstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/pdstack/skills/principle-rich-domain-model))
- **principle-risk-before-coverage**: Apply when you plan, prioritize, select, or cut tests. Spend effort in the order of likelihood times impact and state the risk that you accept. The coverage percentage is a byproduct. It is never the target. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-risk-before-coverage) · ★1)
- **principle-room-to-miss**: Apply when fear of missing is shrinking the attempt. Give the attempt room to fail. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-room-to-miss))
- **principle-root-cause-repair**: Investigate failures or review causal explanations even without a fix request; validate the failed precondition, trace its first broken guarantee, and identify its responsible owner. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-root-cause-repair) · ★1)
- **principle-route-lessons**: 失敗や訂正から得た知見をどこに残すか決めるときに適用する。ワークフロー自身の知見は機構（検査・型・スクリプト・手引き）へ、対象のドメインの知見は対象のリポジトリへ。文書に書くだけで済ませない。 ([B16B1RD/bb-writ](https://github.com/B16B1RD/bb-writ/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-route-lessons))
- **principle-safety-boundaries-are-hard**: Apply before weakening any override rule, approval gate, escalation path, or disclosure requirement. Safety boundaries are not tunable by optimization. ([frinfo702/cowork](https://github.com/frinfo702/cowork/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-safety-boundaries-are-hard))
- **principle-same-instruments**: Apply when the draft changes wording and leaves the method alone. Require the tools the best people in the field use. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-same-instruments))
- **principle-scale-verification**: WHEN choosing which checks to run on a change; NOT for designing a test's assertions or handling a failing gate; runs every required check and scales extra checks to risk and reversibility. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-scale-verification) · ★37)
- **principle-scope-checks-to-the-change**: Apply before any typecheck, test run, lint, or build. Run only the checks that cover what you changed. Whole-repo gates run only when the operator asks for them. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-scope-checks-to-the-change))
- **principle-scope-the-session**: Apply when a session's actual work drifts away from what it was opened to do — a side-investigation becomes the main thread, or an unrelated request gets absorbed into an existing session. Start a fresh session instead of letting scope creep silently expand the one you're in. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-scope-the-session) · ★2)
- **principle-security**: Security design principles — trust boundaries and input validation, authentication vs authorization, secrets handling, secure defaults and defense in depth, lightweight threat modeling, cryptography hygiene, attack-surface minimization. Auto-load when designing auth, discussing authn or authz, handling secrets, defining trust boundaries, validating untrusted input, considering SSRF or CSRF, choosing session or JWT mechanics, configuring TLS, picking an encryption primitive, or weighing least-privilege trade-offs. ([aibot88/sec_skill_store](https://github.com/aibot88/sec_skill_store/tree/HEAD/skills/claudskills/principle-security) · ★6)
- **principle-security**: Security design principles — trust boundaries and input validation, secure defaults and defense in depth, lightweight threat modeling, cryptography hygiene, RBAC/ABAC authorization models, OAuth 2.0/OIDC token validation. Auto-load when designing auth, discussing authn or authz, handling secrets or credentials, defining trust boundaries, validating untrusted input, considering SSRF or CSRF, configuring TLS, weighing least-privilege trade-offs, choosing RBAC or ABAC, handling PII or GDPR data, or pinning deps and generating an SBOM. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-security) · ★3)
- **principle-seen-success**: Apply when a goal looks unreachable and one case of it already exists. Point at that case. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-seen-success))
- **principle-selective-queries**: Apply when writing or reviewing SOQL or SOSL. Every query filters on indexed fields with positive operators, returns only the fields needed, and stays selective at production volume: no leading-wildcard LIKE, no negative operators, no formula fields in WHERE without a custom index. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-selective-queries) · ★1)
- **principle-self-explaining-code**: Apply when writing or reviewing any code. No narrating comments; names, types, and structure carry intent. A comment survives only for a non-obvious why the code cannot show. ([RestartDK/dotfiles](https://github.com/RestartDK/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/config/agents/skills/dstack/principle-self-explaining-code) · ★1)
- **principle-self-explanatory-code**: Code that explains itself, and docs for what it cannot say. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-self-explanatory-code))
- **principle-separate-author-and-verifier**: Apply before declaring agent-written code correct. The agent that wrote a change does not grade it. A fresh agent, ideally on a different model, checks it against the original request with its own evidence. ([Nikshay1/nystack](https://github.com/Nikshay1/nystack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-separate-author-and-verifier))
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/HEAD/harness/pstack/claude-code/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state) · ★17k · 10k installs · 410 copies)
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 10k installs)
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 1k installs)
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Apply when parallel agents, workers, or processes might write the same key, file, state object, or branch. Start by giving each writer its own target, and add locking only where the design truly requires one shared target. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state) · ★1.6k · 4 copies)
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Eliminate unnecessary sharing or partition ownership before adding locks, queues, or coordination. Use when concurrency, locks, queues, shared state, or complex coordination are being considered. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state) · ★117 · 2 copies)
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state) · ★38)
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: WHEN concurrent writers share mutable state; NOT independent read-only data; separate ownership or enforce the shared invariant atomically. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state) · ★37)
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Apply when concurrent actors (tabs, taskpanes, worktrees, agents, cron jobs) might read/write the same token, file, database, or branch. Eliminate sharing first. ([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state) · ★20 · 7 copies)
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Separate Before Serializing Shared State ([PickleBoxer/pstack](https://github.com/PickleBoxer/pstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state) · ★4)
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Separate before serializing shared state. Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key or state object. ([erickb336/sage-stack](https://github.com/erickb336/sage-stack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sage-codex/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**: Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Give each writer its own file, key, or branch first; serialize only a real single-writer invariant.
